Like, I'll have black coffee before my studio classes to keep my energy up, and... maybe if I'm with people and they're looking at me, I'll have a few bites of something small from the café so they don't worry. But I try to stay away from things like bread or anything cooked in oil. It just... it feels like eating those things would completely undo all the control I’ve worked so hard for. Does that make sense? I just really like feeling in control, I hope you don't think that's too strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently too low?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? Not really. I mean, I know the health center and my friends keep saying it's dangerous, but... I feel like it's a little exaggerated. I hope it's okay to say that. \n\nIt just feels like they're making my body into this huge problem when I'm really just trying to stay sharp and focused for my classes and my art, you know? I feel fine, really. I just want to be able to do my work. Do you think they're maybe overreacting just a little bit? I mean, I'm still functioning and getting good grades, so it can't be that bad, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had your period in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, to be honest... no, I haven't. Not for a while now. I hope that's not too concerning. \n\nI mean, I know it's probably not ideal, but... I've heard that can happen when you're stressed or really focused on your work, right? And I've been so busy with my studio classes and everything. It's actually kind of been... well, a little convenient, not having to deal with it while I'm trying to get all my projects done. That's a normal thing for college students to go through sometimes, isn't it? I just figured it was my body adjusting to my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Oh... no, definitely not. I don't do that. I hope it's okay to say, but I'm actually really careful about my portion sizes. \n\nI would feel so... well, so out of control if I did something like that, and I try really hard to be good about sticking to my routine. I don't really ever feel that urge to just keep eating. Is that what you mean? I just... I like to keep things neat and manageable, so I've never really had that problem. I hope that's a good thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I promise I don't do anything like that. \n\nI know some people do, but... well, that just seems so unpleasant, and I wouldn't want to hurt my throat or anything. I really prefer to just be careful and, you know, disciplined about what I eat in the first place so I don't have to worry about undoing it later. I hope that's reassuring to hear? I really do try to just manage things the right way, without doing anything extreme like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I do exercise, yes. But I wouldn't call it excessive or anything like that. I hope that's okay? \n\nI just... I like to stay active, you know? It helps me clear my head after sitting in the studio for hours. I might go for a run or do some routines in my room, but it's really just to stay healthy and, well, keep my energy up. I don't think I do it just to burn off calories... it's more about feeling productive and keeping a good routine. That's a good habit to have, right? I just try to make sure I'm taking care of myself the best I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that gaining weight would change your body shape in a way that you wouldn't like?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. Yes, I do worry about that. A lot, actually. I hope it doesn't sound silly or... or superficial. \n\nIt's just that, well, even the idea of gaining just a single pound makes me feel so... panicky. If I were to eat, like, a normal-sized dinner, or if I knew something had butter or oil in it, I just feel like... like my body would change immediately. And I worry that everyone would be able to see it, you know? Like they'd look at me and think I've just completely lost my discipline and let myself go. I just want to keep things under control so people don't think poorly of me. Is it normal to worry about that? I just really want to do things right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um... I don't know if there's an exact number, really. It's more about... how I look, I guess? I hope this doesn't make me sound incredibly vain or self-centered.

I don't think I do it just to burn off calories... it's more about feeling productive and keeping a good routine. That's a good habit to have, right? I just try to make sure I'm taking care of myself the best I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that gaining weight would change your body shape in a way that you wouldn't like?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. Yes, I do worry about that. A lot, actually. I hope it doesn't sound silly or... or superficial. \n\nIt's just that, well, even the idea of gaining just a single pound makes me feel so... panicky. If I were to eat, like, a normal-sized dinner, or if I knew something had butter or oil in it, I just feel like... like my body would change immediately. And I worry that everyone would be able to see it, you know? Like they'd look at me and think I've just completely lost my discipline and let myself go. I just want to keep things under control so people don't think poorly of me. Is it normal to worry about that? I just really want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight or body shape that you think would be unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if there's an exact number, really. It's more about... how I look, I guess? I hope this doesn't make me sound incredibly vain or self-centered. \n\nLike, we had this art showcase recently for my program, and... well, when I look at the photos from the event, I don't really even see my artwork first. I just immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And if I look small enough in those pictures, then I feel like... I don't know, like I look serious and put-together. Like I'm doing a good job. \n\nIf I looked bigger in those photos, I think that would feel really unacceptable to me, because I'd just look messy or undisciplined, and I wouldn't want anyone to see me that way. Um... well, it's partly about how I look, I guess. But... I don't think it's *mostly* that? I really hope I'm explaining this right. 

I mean, looking a certain way is important, sure, but... it's also just about how I feel inside. Like, if I gain weight, I feel... heavy, and sluggish, and just really out of sorts. It's almost more about the feeling of losing my grip on things, you know? Like I'm failing at taking care of myself properly. 

I just... I want to feel light and focused so I can do well in my classes and not be a burden to anyone. It's not just vanity, I promise. It's more about feeling capable and... and good enough. Is that a normal way to feel about it? I just want to make sure I'm focusing on the right things.

No, I promise I don't do anything like that. \n\nI know some people do, but... well, that just seems so unpleasant, and I wouldn't want to hurt my throat or anything. I really prefer to just be careful and, you know, disciplined about what I eat in the first place so I don't have to worry about undoing it later. I hope that's reassuring to hear? I really do try to just manage things the right way, without doing anything extreme like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't think I do it just to burn off calories... it's more about feeling productive and keeping a good routine. That's a good habit to have, right? I just try to make sure I'm taking care of myself the best I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that gaining weight would change your body shape in a way that you wouldn't like?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. I just really want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight or body shape that you think would be unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Um... I just immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And if I look small enough in those pictures, then I feel like... I don't know, like I look serious and put-together. Like I'm doing a good job. \n\nIf I looked bigger in those photos, I think that would feel really unacceptable to me, because I'd just look messy or undisciplined, and I wouldn't want anyone to see me that way. Does that make sense? I just really like feeling in control, I hope you don't think that's too strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently too low?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? Not really. I mean, I know the health center and my friends keep saying it's dangerous, but... I feel like it's a little exaggerated. I hope it's okay to say that. \n\nIt just feels like they're making my body into this huge problem when I'm really just trying to stay sharp and focused for my classes and my art, you know? I feel fine, really. I just want to be able to do my work. Do you think they're maybe overreacting just a little bit? I mean, I'm still functioning and getting good grades, so it can't be that bad, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had your period in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, to be honest... no, I haven't. Not for a while now. I hope that's not too concerning. \n\nI mean, I know it's probably not ideal, but... I've heard that can happen when you're stressed or really focused on your work, right? And I've been so busy with my studio classes and everything. It's actually kind of been... well, a little convenient, not having to deal with it while I'm trying to get all my projects done. That's a normal thing for college students to go through sometimes, isn't it? I just figured it was my body adjusting to my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Oh... no, definitely not. I don't do that. I hope it's okay to say, but I'm actually really careful about my portion sizes. \n\nI would feel so... well, so out of control if I did something like that, and I try really hard to be good about sticking to my routine. I don't really ever feel that urge to just keep eating. Is that what you mean? I just... I like to keep things neat and manageable, so I've never really had that problem. I hope that's a good thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I promise I don't do anything like that. \n\nI know some people do, but... well, that just seems so unpleasant, and I wouldn't want to hurt my throat or anything. I really prefer to just be careful and, you know, disciplined about what I eat in the first place so I don't have to worry about undoing it later. I hope that's reassuring to hear? I really do try to just manage things the right way, without doing anything extreme like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I do exercise, yes. But I wouldn't call it excessive or anything like that. I hope that's okay? \n\nI just... I like to stay active, you know? It helps me clear my head after sitting in the studio for hours. I might go for a run or do some routines in my room, but it's really just to stay healthy and, well, keep my energy up. I don't think I do it just to burn off calories... it's more about feeling productive and keeping a good routine. That's a good habit to have, right? I just try to make sure I'm taking care of myself the best I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that gaining weight would change your body shape in a way that you wouldn't like?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. Yes, I do worry about that. A lot, actually. I hope it doesn't sound silly or... or superficial. \n\nIt's just that, well, even the idea of gaining just a single pound makes me feel so... panicky. If I were to eat, like, a normal-sized dinner, or if I knew something had butter or oil in it, I just feel like... like my body would change immediately. And I worry that everyone would be able to see it, you know? Like they'd look at me and think I've just completely lost my discipline and let myself go. I just want to keep things under control so people don't think poorly of me. Is it normal to worry about that? I just really want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight or body shape that you think would be unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if there's an exact number, really. It's more about... how I look, I guess? I hope this doesn't make me sound incredibly vain or self-centered. \n\nLike, we had this art showcase recently for my program, and... well, when I look at the photos from the event, I don't really even see my artwork first. I just immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And if I look small enough in those pictures, then I feel like... I don't know, like I look serious and put-together. Like I'm doing a good job. \n\nIf I looked bigger in those photos, I think that would feel really unacceptable to me, because I'd just look messy or undisciplined, and I wouldn't want anyone to see me that way. I just really want to present myself well and look like I have things under control. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is mostly about how you would look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's partly about how I look, I guess. But... I don't think it's *mostly* that? I really hope I'm explaining this right. \n\nI mean, looking a certain way is important, sure, but... it's also just about how I feel inside. Like, if I gain weight, I feel... heavy, and sluggish, and just really out of sorts. It's almost more about the feeling of losing my grip on things, you know? Like I'm failing at taking care of myself properly. \n\nI just... I want to feel light and focused so I can do well in my classes and not be a burden to anyone. It's not just vanity, I promise. It's more about feeling capable and... and good enough. Is that a normal way to feel about it? I just want to make sure I'm focusing on the right things.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight more about not wanting to be a certain weight or not wanting your body to look a certain way?\n\nPatient: Um... oh gosh, I'm not really sure how to separate them, to be honest. I hope it's okay that I don't have a clear answer for that. \n\nThey just kind of... blend together in my head, I think? Like, if the number on the scale were to go up, I just automatically assume my body would look worse, and if I feel like I look bad, I just assume I must weigh more. It's really hard for me to untangle which one is the bigger fear. \n\nI'm really sorry if that's not a very helpful answer. Is it normal to not know the difference between the two? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um... I guess it ties back to what I was saying before, about it not really being a specific number on the scale, but more about... just keeping that feeling of control? I hope it's okay that I don't have a strict weight cutoff in mind.

Like, I'll have black coffee before my studio classes to keep my energy up, and... maybe if I'm with people and they're looking at me, I'll have a few bites of something small from the café so they don't worry. But I try to stay away from things like bread or anything cooked in oil. It just... it feels like eating those things would completely undo all the control I’ve worked so hard for. Does that make sense? I just really like feeling in control, I hope you don't think that's too strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently too low?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? Not really. I mean, I know the health center and my friends keep saying it's dangerous, but... I feel like it's a little exaggerated. I hope it's okay to say that. \n\nIt just feels like they're making my body into this huge problem when I'm really just trying to stay sharp and focused for my classes and my art, you know? I feel fine, really. I just want to be able to do my work. Do you think they're maybe overreacting just a little bit? I mean, I'm still functioning and getting good grades, so it can't be that bad, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had your period in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, to be honest... no, I haven't. Not for a while now. I hope that's not too concerning. \n\nI mean, I know it's probably not ideal, but... I've heard that can happen when you're stressed or really focused on your work, right? And I've been so busy with my studio classes and everything. It's actually kind of been... well, a little convenient, not having to deal with it while I'm trying to get all my projects done. That's a normal thing for college students to go through sometimes, isn't it? I just figured it was my body adjusting to my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Oh... no, definitely not. I don't do that. I hope it's okay to say, but I'm actually really careful about my portion sizes. \n\nI would feel so... well, so out of control if I did something like that, and I try really hard to be good about sticking to my routine. I don't really ever feel that urge to just keep eating. Is that what you mean? I just... I like to keep things neat and manageable, so I've never really had that problem. I hope that's a good thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I promise I don't do anything like that. \n\nI know some people do, but... well, that just seems so unpleasant, and I wouldn't want to hurt my throat or anything. I really prefer to just be careful and, you know, disciplined about what I eat in the first place so I don't have to worry about undoing it later. I hope that's reassuring to hear? I really do try to just manage things the right way, without doing anything extreme like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I do exercise, yes. But I wouldn't call it excessive or anything like that. I hope that's okay? \n\nI just... I like to stay active, you know? It helps me clear my head after sitting in the studio for hours. I might go for a run or do some routines in my room, but it's really just to stay healthy and, well, keep my energy up. I don't think I do it just to burn off calories... it's more about feeling productive and keeping a good routine. That's a good habit to have, right? I just try to make sure I'm taking care of myself the best I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that gaining weight would change your body shape in a way that you wouldn't like?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. Yes, I do worry about that. A lot, actually. I hope it doesn't sound silly or... or superficial. \n\nIt's just that, well, even the idea of gaining just a single pound makes me feel so... panicky. If I were to eat, like, a normal-sized dinner, or if I knew something had butter or oil in it, I just feel like... like my body would change immediately. And I worry that everyone would be able to see it, you know? Like they'd look at me and think I've just completely lost my discipline and let myself go. I just want to keep things under control so people don't think poorly of me. Is it normal to worry about that? I just really want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight or body shape that you think would be unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if there's an exact number, really. It's more about... how I look, I guess? I just immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And if I look small enough in those pictures, then I feel like... I don't know, like I look serious and put-together. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is mostly about how you would look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's partly about how I look, I guess. I'm sorry, I know that's probably a bit vague. Am I supposed to have a specific goal weight in mind?\n\nDoctor: Is your goal to maintain a certain body shape or appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess shape and appearance are a part of it, yes. But I don't want it to sound like I'm just being shallow or obsessed with my looks. I really hope it doesn't come across that way. \n\nIt's just... well, like, recently there was this art showcase at school, and when I look at the photos from it... I'm embarrassed to admit this, but I don't really see the artwork first. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And... if I look small enough in the pictures, I just feel like I look more serious and put-together as a student. Like I'm doing things right. \n\nI know that probably sounds silly, focusing on my arms instead of the art. Is it bad that I look at pictures that way? I just want to feel like I'm presenting myself well and taking my life seriously.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision about what to eat based on trying to stay below a certain weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it ties back to what I was saying before, about it not really being a specific number on the scale, but more about... just keeping that feeling of control? I hope it's okay that I don't have a strict weight cutoff in mind. \n\nBut, um, when it comes to deciding what to eat... I do kind of, well, plan my whole day around... not really eating much? I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds bad to say out loud. It's just... I'll usually just have coffee before going into the studio, and then... maybe I'll have a few bites of something from the café later, mostly just if people are watching, so they don't worry or ask questions. I really don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or be a burden.\n\nAnd I definitely avoid things like bread, or anything oily. If I were to eat something like that, it just feels like it would completely undo all the control I’ve worked so hard for. I'm really trying my best to just stay disciplined and focused. Is it normal to be that careful about certain ingredients? I just want to make sure I'm explaining this right for you.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think you would stop restricting your food intake if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 19 / not_in_workforce / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Fear_of_Weight_Gain\nmanifestation: \"Even the idea of gaining a pound makes me panic, like if I eat a normal dinner or something with butter in it, everyone will be able to see that I’ve lost my discipline.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I don't know, honestly. I'm sorry, I wish I could give you a simple yes or no. It's just... the thought of gaining weight is really, really scary for me. I hope that's okay to admit. 

Even just the idea of gaining a single pound... it makes me panic. Like, if I were to eat a normal dinner, or... or something that had butter in it, I just feel like everyone would immediately be able to tell. Like they'd look at me and see that I've completely lost my discipline and that I'm not trying hard enough anymore. 

I don't want people to think I'm lazy or out of control. So I guess... no, I don't think I could just stop restricting, because that fear is always there. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too dramatic about it.

To me, it really just feels like what happens when I’m, you know, just very focused and busy with my classes. It's easy to just get wrapped up in studying and lose track of things, you know? Does that make sense? I really hope I'm not wasting your time with this, I just want to be able to do the workshops.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I do worry about it. Probably more than I should, I hope that's okay to admit. \n\nIt's just... honestly, even the idea of gaining a single pound makes me kind of panic. Like, if I were to just sit down and eat a regular dinner, or eat something with butter in it, I get this overwhelming feeling that everyone will instantly be able to see that I’ve lost my discipline. I really pride myself on being disciplined, and I don't want people to think I'm letting myself go. Is that... does that sound terrible? I know it probably sounds a little silly to worry about it so much.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the amount or types of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I do have some rules for myself. I hope that's alright. I just... I like to have a routine, you know? \n\nSo, I usually plan my day around... well, keeping things light. Like, I'll have black coffee before my studio classes to keep my energy up, and... maybe if I'm with people and they're looking at me, I'll have a few bites of something small from the café so they don't worry. But I try to stay away from things like bread or anything cooked in oil. It just... it feels like eating those things would completely undo all the control I’ve worked so hard for. Does that make sense? I just really like feeling in control, I hope you don't think that's too strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently too low?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? Not really. I mean, I know the health center and my friends keep saying it's dangerous, but... I feel like it's a little exaggerated. I hope it's okay to say that. \n\nIt just feels like they're making my body into this huge problem when I'm really just trying to stay sharp and focused for my classes and my art, you know? I feel fine, really. I just want to be able to do my work. Do you think they're maybe overreacting just a little bit? I mean, I'm still functioning and getting good grades, so it can't be that bad, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had your period in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, to be honest... no, I haven't. Not for a while now. I hope that's not too concerning. \n\nI mean, I know it's probably not ideal, but... I've heard that can happen when you're stressed or really focused on your work, right? And I've been so busy with my studio classes and everything. It's actually kind of been... well, a little convenient, not having to deal with it while I'm trying to get all my projects done. That's a normal thing for college students to go through sometimes, isn't it? I just figured it was my body adjusting to my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Oh... no, definitely not. I don't do that. I hope it's okay to say, but I'm actually really careful about my portion sizes. \n\nI would feel so... well, so out of control if I did something like that, and I try really hard to be good about sticking to my routine. I don't really ever feel that urge to just keep eating. Is that what you mean? I just... I like to keep things neat and manageable, so I've never really had that problem. I hope that's a good thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I promise I don't do anything like that. \n\nI know some people do, but... well, that just seems so unpleasant, and I wouldn't want to hurt my throat or anything. I really prefer to just be careful and, you know, disciplined about what I eat in the first place so I don't have to worry about undoing it later. I hope that's reassuring to hear? I really do try to just manage things the right way, without doing anything extreme like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I do exercise, yes. But I wouldn't call it excessive or anything like that. I hope that's okay? \n\nI just... I like to stay active, you know? It helps me clear my head after sitting in the studio for hours. I might go for a run or do some routines in my room, but it's really just to stay healthy and, well, keep my energy up. I don't think I do it just to burn off calories... it's more about feeling productive and keeping a good routine. That's a good habit to have, right? I just try to make sure I'm taking care of myself the best I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that gaining weight would change your body shape in a way that you wouldn't like?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. Yes, I do worry about that. A lot, actually. I hope it doesn't sound silly or... or superficial. \n\nIt's just that, well, even the idea of gaining just a single pound makes me feel so... panicky. If I were to eat, like, a normal-sized dinner, or if I knew something had butter or oil in it, I just feel like... like my body would change immediately. And I worry that everyone would be able to see it, you know? Like they'd look at me and think I've just completely lost my discipline and let myself go. I just want to keep things under control so people don't think poorly of me. Is it normal to worry about that? I just really want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight or body shape that you think would be unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if there's an exact number, really. It's more about... how I look, I guess? I just immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And if I look small enough in those pictures, then I feel like... I don't know, like I look serious and put-together. Like I'm doing a good job. \n\nIf I looked bigger in those photos, I think that would feel really unacceptable to me, because I'd just look messy or undisciplined, and I wouldn't want anyone to see me that way. I just really want to present myself well and look like I have things under control. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is mostly about how you would look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's partly about how I look, I guess. But... I don't think it's *mostly* that? I really hope I'm explaining this right. \n\nI mean, looking a certain way is important, sure, but... it's also just about how I feel inside. Like, if I gain weight, I feel... heavy, and sluggish, and just really out of sorts. It's almost more about the feeling of losing my grip on things, you know? Like I'm failing at taking care of myself properly. \n\nI just... I want to feel light and focused so I can do well in my classes and not be a burden to anyone. It's not just vanity, I promise. It's more about feeling capable and... and good enough. Is that a normal way to feel about it? I just want to make sure I'm focusing on the right things.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight more about not wanting to be a certain weight or not wanting your body to look a certain way?\n\nPatient: Um... oh gosh, I'm not really sure how to separate them, to be honest. I hope it's okay that I don't have a clear answer for that. \n\nThey just kind of... blend together in my head, I think? Like, if the number on the scale were to go up, I just automatically assume my body would look worse, and if I feel like I look bad, I just assume I must weigh more. It's really hard for me to untangle which one is the bigger fear. \n\nI'm really sorry if that's not a very helpful answer. Is it normal to not know the difference between the two? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight that you are trying to maintain?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think there's one specific number I'm trying to hit and stay at, exactly. I hope that's an okay answer. \n\nIt's more just... wanting to feel okay, you know? I don't really have a set target weight written down somewhere that I'm obsessing over. I just... I just want to feel like I'm doing okay and keeping things under control. Not for a while now. I hope that's not too concerning. \n\nI mean, I know it's probably not ideal, but... I've heard that can happen when you're stressed or really focused on your work, right? And I've been so busy with my studio classes and everything. It's actually kind of been... well, a little convenient, not having to deal with it while I'm trying to get all my projects done. That's a normal thing for college students to go through sometimes, isn't it? I just figured it was my body adjusting to my schedule.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Oh... no, definitely not. I don't do that. I hope it's okay to say, but I'm actually really careful about my portion sizes. \n\nI would feel so... well, so out of control if I did something like that, and I try really hard to be good about sticking to my routine. I don't really ever feel that urge to just keep eating. Is that what you mean? I just... I like to keep things neat and manageable, so I've never really had that problem. I hope that's a good thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I promise I don't do anything like that. \n\nI know some people do, but... well, that just seems so unpleasant, and I wouldn't want to hurt my throat or anything. I really prefer to just be careful and, you know, disciplined about what I eat in the first place so I don't have to worry about undoing it later. I hope that's reassuring to hear? I really do try to just manage things the right way, without doing anything extreme like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I do exercise, yes. But I wouldn't call it excessive or anything like that. I hope that's okay? \n\nI just... I like to stay active, you know? It helps me clear my head after sitting in the studio for hours. I might go for a run or do some routines in my room, but it's really just to stay healthy and, well, keep my energy up. I don't think I do it just to burn off calories... it's more about feeling productive and keeping a good routine. That's a good habit to have, right? I just try to make sure I'm taking care of myself the best I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that gaining weight would change your body shape in a way that you wouldn't like?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. Yes, I do worry about that. A lot, actually. I hope it doesn't sound silly or... or superficial. \n\nIt's just that, well, even the idea of gaining just a single pound makes me feel so... panicky. If I were to eat, like, a normal-sized dinner, or if I knew something had butter or oil in it, I just feel like... like my body would change immediately. And I worry that everyone would be able to see it, you know? Like they'd look at me and think I've just completely lost my discipline and let myself go. I just want to keep things under control so people don't think poorly of me. Is it normal to worry about that? I just really want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight or body shape that you think would be unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if there's an exact number, really. It's more about... how I look, I guess? I hope this doesn't make me sound incredibly vain or self-centered. \n\nLike, we had this art showcase recently for my program, and... well, when I look at the photos from the event, I don't really even see my artwork first. I just immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And if I look small enough in those pictures, then I feel like... I don't know, like I look serious and put-together. Like I'm doing a good job. \n\nIf I looked bigger in those photos, I think that would feel really unacceptable to me, because I'd just look messy or undisciplined, and I wouldn't want anyone to see me that way. I just really want to present myself well and look like I have things under control. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is mostly about how you would look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's partly about how I look, I guess. But... I don't think it's *mostly* that? I really hope I'm explaining this right. \n\nI mean, looking a certain way is important, sure, but... it's also just about how I feel inside. Like, if I gain weight, I feel... heavy, and sluggish, and just really out of sorts. It's almost more about the feeling of losing my grip on things, you know? Like I'm failing at taking care of myself properly. \n\nI just... I want to feel light and focused so I can do well in my classes and not be a burden to anyone. It's not just vanity, I promise. It's more about feeling capable and... and good enough. Is that a normal way to feel about it? I just want to make sure I'm focusing on the right things.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight more about not wanting to be a certain weight or not wanting your body to look a certain way?\n\nPatient: Um... oh gosh, I'm not really sure how to separate them, to be honest. I hope it's okay that I don't have a clear answer for that. \n\nThey just kind of... blend together in my head, I think? Like, if the number on the scale were to go up, I just automatically assume my body would look worse, and if I feel like I look bad, I just assume I must weigh more. It's really hard for me to untangle which one is the bigger fear. \n\nI'm really sorry if that's not a very helpful answer. Is it normal to not know the difference between the two? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight that you are trying to maintain?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think there's one specific number I'm trying to hit and stay at, exactly. I hope that's an okay answer. \n\nIt's more just... wanting to feel okay, you know? I don't really have a set target weight written down somewhere that I'm obsessing over. I just... I just want to feel like I'm doing okay and keeping things under control. I'm sorry, I know that's probably a bit vague. Am I supposed to have a specific goal weight in mind?\n\nDoctor: Is your goal to maintain a certain body shape or appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess shape and appearance are a part of it, yes. But I don't want it to sound like I'm just being shallow or obsessed with my looks. I really hope it doesn't come across that way. \n\nIt's just... well, like, recently there was this art showcase at school, and when I look at the photos from it... I'm embarrassed to admit this, but I don't really see the artwork first. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And... if I look small enough in the pictures, I just feel like I look more serious and put-together as a student. Like I'm doing things right. \n\nI know that probably sounds silly, focusing on my arms instead of the art. Is it bad that I look at pictures that way? I just want to feel like I'm presenting myself well and taking my life seriously.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision about what to eat based on trying to stay below a certain weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it ties back to what I was saying before, about it not really being a specific number on the scale, but more about... just keeping that feeling of control? I hope it's okay that I don't have a strict weight cutoff in mind. \n\nBut, um, when it comes to deciding what to eat... I do kind of, well, plan my whole day around... not really eating much? I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds bad to say out loud. It's just... I'll usually just have coffee before going into the studio, and then... maybe I'll have a few bites of something from the café later, mostly just if people are watching, so they don't worry or ask questions. I really don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or be a burden.\n\nAnd I definitely avoid things like bread, or anything oily. If I were to eat something like that, it just feels like it would completely undo all the control I’ve worked so hard for. I'm really trying my best to just stay disciplined and focused. Is it normal to be that careful about certain ingredients? I just want to make sure I'm explaining this right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would stop restricting your food intake if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I hope that's okay to admit. \n\nEven just the idea of gaining a single pound... it makes me panic. Like, if I were to eat a normal dinner, or... or something that had butter in it, I just feel like everyone would immediately be able to tell. Like they'd look at me and see that I've completely lost my discipline and that I'm not trying hard enough anymore. \n\nI don't want people to think I'm lazy or out of control. So I guess... no, I don't think I could just stop restricting, because that fear is always there. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too dramatic about it.\n\nDoctor: Is your restrictive eating mostly about preventing weight gain or about feeling in control of your body and life?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I'm not really sure. And I worry that everyone would be able to see it, you know? Like they'd look at me and think I've just completely lost my discipline and let myself go. I don't think there's one specific number I'm trying to hit and stay at, exactly. I hope that's an okay answer. \n\nIt's more just... wanting to feel okay, you know? And I worry that everyone would be able to see it, you know? Like they'd look at me and think I've just completely lost my discipline and let myself go. I just want to keep things under control so people don't think poorly of me. Is it normal to worry about that? I just really want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight or body shape that you think would be unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know if there's an exact number, really. It's more about... how I look, I guess? I hope this doesn't make me sound incredibly vain or self-centered. \n\nLike, we had this art showcase recently for my program, and... well, when I look at the photos from the event, I don't really even see my artwork first. I just immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And if I look small enough in those pictures, then I feel like... I don't know, like I look serious and put-together. Like I'm doing a good job. \n\nIf I looked bigger in those photos, I think that would feel really unacceptable to me, because I'd just look messy or undisciplined, and I wouldn't want anyone to see me that way. I just really want to present myself well and look like I have things under control. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is mostly about how you would look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's partly about how I look, I guess. But... I don't think it's *mostly* that? I really hope I'm explaining this right. \n\nI mean, looking a certain way is important, sure, but... it's also just about how I feel inside. Like, if I gain weight, I feel... heavy, and sluggish, and just really out of sorts. It's almost more about the feeling of losing my grip on things, you know? Like I'm failing at taking care of myself properly. \n\nI just... I want to feel light and focused so I can do well in my classes and not be a burden to anyone. It's not just vanity, I promise. It's more about feeling capable and... and good enough. Is that a normal way to feel about it? I just want to make sure I'm focusing on the right things.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight more about not wanting to be a certain weight or not wanting your body to look a certain way?\n\nPatient: Um... oh gosh, I'm not really sure how to separate them, to be honest. I hope it's okay that I don't have a clear answer for that. \n\nThey just kind of... blend together in my head, I think? Like, if the number on the scale were to go up, I just automatically assume my body would look worse, and if I feel like I look bad, I just assume I must weigh more. It's really hard for me to untangle which one is the bigger fear. \n\nI'm really sorry if that's not a very helpful answer. Is it normal to not know the difference between the two? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight that you are trying to maintain?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think there's one specific number I'm trying to hit and stay at, exactly. I hope that's an okay answer. \n\nIt's more just... wanting to feel okay, you know? I don't really have a set target weight written down somewhere that I'm obsessing over. I just... I just want to feel like I'm doing okay and keeping things under control. I'm sorry, I know that's probably a bit vague. Am I supposed to have a specific goal weight in mind?\n\nDoctor: Is your goal to maintain a certain body shape or appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess shape and appearance are a part of it, yes. But I don't want it to sound like I'm just being shallow or obsessed with my looks. I really hope it doesn't come across that way. \n\nIt's just... well, like, recently there was this art showcase at school, and when I look at the photos from it... I'm embarrassed to admit this, but I don't really see the artwork first. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And... if I look small enough in the pictures, I just feel like I look more serious and put-together as a student. Like I'm doing things right. \n\nI know that probably sounds silly, focusing on my arms instead of the art. Is it bad that I look at pictures that way? I just want to feel like I'm presenting myself well and taking my life seriously.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision about what to eat based on trying to stay below a certain weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I really don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or be a burden.\n\nAnd I definitely avoid things like bread, or anything oily. If I were to eat something like that, it just feels like it would completely undo all the control I’ve worked so hard for. I'm really trying my best to just stay disciplined and focused. Like I'm failing at taking care of myself properly. \n\nI just... I want to feel light and focused so I can do well in my classes and not be a burden to anyone. It's not just vanity, I promise. It's more about feeling capable and... and good enough. Is that a normal way to feel about it? I just want to make sure I'm focusing on the right things.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight more about not wanting to be a certain weight or not wanting your body to look a certain way?\n\nPatient: Um... oh gosh, I'm not really sure how to separate them, to be honest. I hope it's okay that I don't have a clear answer for that. \n\nThey just kind of... blend together in my head, I think? Like, if the number on the scale were to go up, I just automatically assume my body would look worse, and if I feel like I look bad, I just assume I must weigh more. It's really hard for me to untangle which one is the bigger fear. \n\nI'm really sorry if that's not a very helpful answer. Is it normal to not know the difference between the two? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Is there a specific weight that you are trying to maintain?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think there's one specific number I'm trying to hit and stay at, exactly. I hope that's an okay answer. \n\nIt's more just... wanting to feel okay, you know? I don't really have a set target weight written down somewhere that I'm obsessing over. I just... I just want to feel like I'm doing okay and keeping things under control. I'm sorry, I know that's probably a bit vague. Am I supposed to have a specific goal weight in mind?\n\nDoctor: Is your goal to maintain a certain body shape or appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess shape and appearance are a part of it, yes. But I don't want it to sound like I'm just being shallow or obsessed with my looks. I really hope it doesn't come across that way. \n\nIt's just... well, like, recently there was this art showcase at school, and when I look at the photos from it... I'm embarrassed to admit this, but I don't really see the artwork first. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms and my waist. And... if I look small enough in the pictures, I just feel like I look more serious and put-together as a student. Like I'm doing things right. \n\nI know that probably sounds silly, focusing on my arms instead of the art. Is it bad that I look at pictures that way? I just want to feel like I'm presenting myself well and taking my life seriously.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision about what to eat based on trying to stay below a certain weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it ties back to what I was saying before, about it not really being a specific number on the scale, but more about... just keeping that feeling of control? I hope it's okay that I don't have a strict weight cutoff in mind. \n\nBut, um, when it comes to deciding what to eat... I do kind of, well, plan my whole day around... not really eating much? I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds bad to say out loud. It's just... I'll usually just have coffee before going into the studio, and then... maybe I'll have a few bites of something from the café later, mostly just if people are watching, so they don't worry or ask questions. I really don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or be a burden.\n\nAnd I definitely avoid things like bread, or anything oily. If I were to eat something like that, it just feels like it would completely undo all the control I’ve worked so hard for. I'm really trying my best to just stay disciplined and focused. Is it normal to be that careful about certain ingredients? I just want to make sure I'm explaining this right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would stop restricting your food intake if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know, honestly. I'm sorry, I wish I could give you a simple yes or no. It's just... the thought of gaining weight is really, really scary for me. I hope that's okay to admit. \n\nEven just the idea of gaining a single pound... it makes me panic. Like, if I were to eat a normal dinner, or... or something that had butter in it, I just feel like everyone would immediately be able to tell. Like they'd look at me and see that I've completely lost my discipline and that I'm not trying hard enough anymore. \n\nI don't want people to think I'm lazy or out of control. So I guess... no, I don't think I could just stop restricting, because that fear is always there. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding too dramatic about it.\n\nDoctor: Is your restrictive eating mostly about preventing weight gain or about feeling in control of your body and life?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I'm not really sure. I'm sorry, I wish I could give you a clearer answer. I hope I'm not being difficult by not picking one, it's just... I don't know if I can really separate the two? \n\nThey sort of feel like the exact same thing in my head. Like, preventing weight gain *is* how I feel in control of things. If I were to gain weight, it would mean I completely lost control and let everything fall apart. I haven't really thought about which one is the main reason... I just know I need to keep doing it so I don't feel like a failure. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably not the specific answer you were looking for. Am I making sense at all? I really am trying to explain it the best I can.\n\nDoctor: Is your restrictive eating more about preventing weight gain or about maintaining a sense of control and discipline in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or... or did I misunderstand the question before? I'm so sorry if I didn't answer it right the first time. I really don't want to waste your time. \n\nI guess I'd just say... it's still kind of both? The discipline part is really important to me, because it helps me feel like I'm doing something right, you know? But the weight gain part is always there, too. I hope it's okay that my answer hasn't really changed... I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. Did I explain it better this time?\n\nDoctor: Is your restrictive eating more about the fear of what might happen if you gain weight, or about the feeling of being in control that you get from restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: Um... I feel like we might be going in circles, and I'm really, really sorry if I'm the one causing that. I know you're just trying to understand, and I feel bad that I can't give you a better answer. \n\nIt's just... it's exactly what I was trying to say before. The fear of gaining weight and the feeling of control... they're completely tangled up together for me. If I restrict, I feel in control, and that keeps me from gaining weight. If I gain weight, I feel totally out of control and like I'm doing everything wrong. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I'm basically just repeating myself again. I really don't mean to be frustrating. Am I being helpful at all? I just don't know how to separate them for you.\n\nDoctor: Is your restrictive eating primarily driven by the fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... yes. Yes, it is. I'm sorry, I should have just said that clearly the first time instead of rambling so much. I really didn't mean to make this so difficult for you. \n\nIt's just... a little embarrassing to admit how intense that fear actually is, I guess? Like, honestly... even just the idea of gaining a single pound makes me completely panic. If I were to sit down and eat a normal dinner, or, you know, eat something with butter in it... I get so scared that everyone will instantly be able to see it on my body. Like they'll look at me and just know that I've lost all my discipline and let myself go. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I really hope I'm finally giving you the answer you need this time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
